In a WORKFLOW\\n  document that is the only form: the capture selectors `run text \\\"…\\\"` and\\n  `run json \\\"…\\\"` are worker-document vocabulary (§16) and are refused here,\\n  because the server's dispatcher produces the outcome record and nothing\\n  else.\\n- **Interpolation is the action's own parameters only**, as `$name` or\\n  `${name}`, in snake_case. `$HOME` is refused: a shell variable is not an AWL\\n  parameter. A value is substituted as one WHOLE argv element and never\\n  re-enters a parser, so a value can never become code.\\n- **The program itself must be written out.** `run \\\"$program arg\\\"` is refused.\\n- **The result type is a record over a SUBSET of the fixed outcome object**\\n  `{ exit_code: Int, stdout: String, stderr: String }` — exactly those names,\\n  exactly those types. A non-record return, an unknown member, or a member with\\n  the wrong type is refused.\\n- Shell syntax in the command does NOT work: `run \\\"a | b\\\"` passes `|` and `b`\\n  as arguments to `a`; `run \\\"x > f\\\"` does not redirect.\\n\\n**Read `docs/workers/DECLARED-COMMANDS.md` before writing one. THERE IS NO SANDBOX** —\\nno allowlist, no filesystem confinement, no network restriction, no privilege\\ndrop, no resource limit. A declared command can do anything the server's OS\\nuser can do. What protects you is review, not runtime enforcement: the command\\nis source you can read before deploying, and it is sealed into the package's\\nworker contract, so it cannot change afterwards without producing a different\\npackage.\\n\\nAn action with NO body and no `agent` marker is a requirement on an out-of-band\\nworker. See `WORKERS.md` for the implementation layers.\\n\\n## 5. Children and subflows\\n\\nA child is a separate durable workflow:\\n\\n```awl\\nchild review_round(repo: String, round: Int) -> Report\\n\\nstep review\\n  review_round(repo: request.repo, round: 1) -> report\\n```\\n\\nThe call waits for the child's result. `spawn review_round(...)` starts a child\\nwithout waiting and cannot bind a result. Child deployment and schema matching\\nare runtime/deploy concerns.\\n\\nA subflow is an inline reusable graph:\\n\\n```awl\\nsubflow review_once(repo: String)\\n  outcome out: type Report\\n\\n  step run\\n    inspect(repo: repo) -> report\\n    route out(report)\\n```\\n\\nA subflow has typed parameters, exactly one typed outcome, and its own steps.\\nAn invocation runs as its own step: the call must be the ONLY statement of a\\ntop-level step body — never beside other statements, never inside a `fork`,\\n`loop`, `on failure` block, or substep, and never a pipe stage — and binds the\\noutcome value with `->` (`flow-shape/valid/dev_flow.awl`). It compiles into\\nthe containing workflow; it is not independently deployed or observed.\\nSubflows may nest but cannot recurse.\\n\\nNested `step` declarations are also accepted inside a step body. They share the\\nparent's inputs and form a scoped graph; routes may not escape that scope.\\nSubsteps compile only as the TRAILING block of an ordinary step body — every\\nstatement after the first substep must be another substep — and the parent\\nstep must carry outcome clauses to route on after the group settles. A substep\\ncannot nest further substeps, cannot carry `after` dependencies or a\\n`max … visits` bound, and cannot sit inside a loop body, fork branch, or\\n`on failure` block. These substep shape rules are enforced when the document\\nis compiled, not by `aion awl check` (§15).\\n\\n## 6. Steps, dependencies, and calls\\n\\nStatements in an ordinary step run in written order. A step with no `after` and\\nno incoming route falls through from the preceding written step. `after`\\ncreates explicit dependencies:\\n\\n```awl\\nstep build after prepare\\nstep verify after build, policy\\n```\\n\\nA step starts after all named dependencies complete. Independent steps that\\nbecome eligible together compile to one parallel dispatch only when EVERY\\nmember of the layer is a pure dispatch: no outcome clauses, no `on failure`,\\nno `max … visits`, a body of exactly one call statement, and that call naming\\na declared action — not a child, not an advisory action. A layer with any\\nother member runs in written order instead. The checker rejects unknown\\ntargets, cycles through `after`, unreachable steps, and bindings that are not\\navailable on every path.\\n\\nBind a call with `->`. Pipe a value with `|>`:\\n\\n```awl\\nrequest |> normalize |> .repo |> inspect -> report\\nreport |> route done\\n```\\n\\nA pipe action takes the piped value as its one argument, so a stage must be\\ndeclared with exactly one parameter — a multi-parameter action or child cannot\\nbe a stage, and neither can an advisory action\\n(`declarations/invalid/advisory_pipe_stage.awl`). A plain expression also\\nbinds as a statement with the same arrow — `\\\"run: \\\" + result -> summary`\\n(`ergonomics/valid/flow_vocab_b1.awl`). Available deterministic\\nvalue operations include field access, literal indexing (`items[0]`), string\\n`+`, comparisons, boolean `not`/`and`/`or`, and collection operations\\n`filter`, `map`, `sort`, `count`, `any`, and `all`. Predicates include\\n`is empty`, `is present`, and `is absent`. There are no user functions or\\ngeneral arithmetic; put computation in a worker action.\\n\\n### Guards narrow optionals\\n\\nA guard that proves an access path present narrows that path from `T?` to `T`\\nwherever the proof holds. Within one boolean chain, `and` proves each\\nconjunct's `is present` for the operands after it, so\\n`when nudge.end is present and nudge.end` typechecks, and proofs chain — `a is\\npresent and a.b is present` narrows `a.b` because the first proof already made\\n`a` a record (`step-bodies/valid/guard_chain_carries_every_proof.awl`). `or`\\nproves each disjunct's `is absent` on its false edge:\\n`when detail is absent or detail.approved` reads `.approved` only where\\n`detail` is present (`schema-doors/valid/short_circuit_optional.awl`).\\n\\nAn outcome guard's proofs hold through its whole arm, including the route\\npayload (`step-bodies/valid/guard_narrows_an_optional_field.awl` and\\n`guard_narrows_a_deep_optional_field.awl`). Narrowing keys on the exact access\\npath, not just on whole bindings. Proofs never escape their arm and never flow\\nbackwards to statements before the guard; an optional used without a proof is\\na check error (`loop-outcomes/invalid/optional_used_unguarded.awl`,\\n`step-bodies/invalid/narrowing_does_not_escape_its_arm.awl`,\\n`step-bodies/invalid/proof_does_not_flow_backwards.awl`).\\n\\n## 7. Selection, outcomes, and routes\\n\\nThere is no `select` keyword. A checked selection is an ordered set of step\\noutcomes:\\n\\n```awl\\nstep choose\\n  outcome blocked: when findings |> any(.blocking), route remediation\\n  outcome clear: otherwise,\\n    route done(summary: \\\"all clear\\\")\\n```\\n\\nThe first true `when` wins. `otherwise` is the complement and must be last.\\nConditional outcomes must be exhaustive. Exhaustive normally means an\\n`otherwise` arm, with one proved exception: when every arm's guard compares\\nthe SAME subject against a bare enum variant and every variant of the enum is\\ncovered, the set is total and needs no `otherwise`\\n(`loop-outcomes/valid/enum_when_totality.awl`); an uncovered variant is a\\ncheck error naming that variant\\n(`loop-outcomes/invalid/enum_variant_uncovered.awl`). Enum variants are\\nreferenced bare in expressions as `TitleCase` names — `result.category ==\\nUrgent`. A route names either another step or\\na workflow/subflow outcome. A body-less step containing only outcomes is a pure\\ndecision. A route can also be an ordinary final statement:\\n\\n```awl\\nroute next_step\\n```\\n\\nBackward routes are flow loops and require a real bound with `max N visits` on\\na step in the cycle. The `visits` binding is available in guards.\\n\\n## 8. Parallel and sequential work\\n\\n### Intra-step `fork` / `join`\\n\\nCollection form:\\n\\n```awl\\nfork item in items\\n  inspect(repo: item.repo)\\njoin -> reports\\n```\\n\\nThe collection form runs one branch per item in parallel and joins results in\\ninput order. Its body compiles only as exactly one unbound call, which may\\nname an action or a child — a per-item child fan-out is a checked, lowered\\nshape (`dag-fork/valid/child_collection_fork.awl`). Add `sequential` to run\\none item at a time in input order:\\n\\n```awl\\nfork item in items sequential\\n  publish(repo: item.repo)\\njoin -> receipts\\n```\\n\\nA bare `fork` — the header with no collection — opens named branches, which\\nrun heterogeneous calls concurrently and use `join` without a collection\\nbinding:\\n\\n```awl\\nfork\\n  fetch_profile(id: request.id) -> profile\\n  fetch_history(id: request.id) -> history\\njoin\\n```\\n\\nA named branch compiles only as an action call (a result binding is fine);\\nchild calls in named branches do not lower. Both fork body shapes are\\ncompile-time rules, not check-time (§15).\\n\\n### Multi-step `distribute` / `sequence` regions\\n\\nUse a region when every item needs a graph of steps:\\n\\n```awl\\nstep open\\n  distribute item in items\\n\\nstep work\\n  inspect(repo: item.repo) -> report\\n\\nstep close\\n  collect report -> reports\\n```\\n\\n`distribute` executes item tracks in parallel. Replace it with `sequence` to\\nexecute tracks one at a time in collection order. The opener's region operation\\nmust be its only statement. `collect` must be the FIRST statement of the close\\nstep and closes the nearest region — further statements may follow it\\n(`flow-shape/valid/sequence_region_loopback.awl` counts and judges the\\ncollected list in the same step). Results preserve input order; an empty\\ninput collects to `[]`.\\n\\nStrict `collect report -> reports` fails the run when an item fails. Tolerant\\n`collect report? -> reports` binds `reports` as `[Report?]` — one optional\\nslot per input item, in item order, with failure detail kept in history. That\\nis the one way the `[T?]` shape exists (§2); each slot is an optional and must\\nbe proved present before its fields are read, exactly like any other `T?`\\n(§6). Regions may nest, but each inner region must\\nclose before its outer region; routes cannot enter or escape an open region.\\n\\n## 9. Bounded iteration\\n\\nValue-threading loop:\\n\\n```awl\\nloop round = initial counting cycles\\n  improve(prior: round) -> round\\n  until round.complete\\n  max max_rounds\\n```\\n\\nThe body runs at least once and must rebind the one threaded value. `max` is\\nmandatory. `counting` is optional and binds the number of completed iterations.\\nA step containing a loop needs explicit outcomes that distinguish completion\\nfrom exhaustion. `route` is not allowed inside the loop body.\\n\\nFlow loops use backward routes plus a step-level visit bound:\\n\\n```awl\\nstep review\\n  inspect(repo: request.repo) -> report\\n  outcome again: when report.blocking, route revise\\n  outcome done: otherwise, route finished(report)\\n  max 3 visits\\n```\\n\\nThe visit bound is an expression over inputs and consts, not only a literal —\\n`max max_polish_rounds visits`\\n(`loop-outcomes/valid/backward_route_bounded_cycle.awl`).\\n\\nUse the shape that matches the work; never emulate an unbounded loop.\\n\\n## 10. Waits, timeouts, signals, and sleep\\n\\nDeclare a signal in the header, then wait for it:\\n\\n```awl\\nstep hold\\n  wait approval -> answer\\n```\\n\\nThe binding is mandatory: `wait approval` without `-> answer` is a parse error\\n(`step-bodies/invalid/wait_missing_binding.awl`).\\n\\nA timed wait binds an optional value because expiry is absence:\\n\\n```awl\\nstep hold\\n  wait approval timeout 2h -> answer\\n  outcome approved: when answer is present, route continue_work\\n  outcome expired: otherwise, route failed(reason: \\\"approval timed out\\\")\\n```\\n\\n`sleep 30s` is a durable timer statement. Action timeout belongs on an action or\\ncall; workflow timeout belongs in the header; wait timeout belongs on `wait`.\\nThey are distinct.\\n\\n## 11. Failure handling\\n\\nAn action failure fails its step after declared retries. Optional compensation:\\n\\n```awl\\non failure\\n  remove_assets(ids: assets)\\n  route failed(reason: \\\"publish failed after compensation\\\")\\n```\\n\\n`on failure` must end in a route; silently swallowing failure is invalid.\\nExternal-command exit status may instead be modeled as ordinary typed action\\ndata when the workflow needs to decide what it means.\\n\\n## 12. Sending signals to other workflows\\n\\n`wait` receives a signal. `send` is the other direction: this workflow signals\\nanother running workflow, addressed by its durable id.\\n\\nDeclare what you send in the workflow header, then send it from a step body:\\n\\n```awl\\n//! Announce an item to its coordinator as it is gated.\\nworkflow gated_item\\n  input coordinator_id: String\\n  input item_id: String\\n  send heartbeat: Nil\\n  send progress: Progress\\n  send verdict: String\\n  outcome done: type Progress, route success\\n\\ntype Progress { item: String, state: String }\\n\\nstep announce\\n  send heartbeat to coordinator_id\\n  send progress(item: item_id, state: \\\"started\\\") to coordinator_id\\n  send verdict(\\\"gated: \\\" + item_id) to coordinator_id\\n  send progress(item: item_id, state: \\\"gated\\\") to workflow.id\\n  route done(item: item_id, state: \\\"gated\\\")\\n```\\n\\nThe payload takes the same three forms a route payload does:\\n\\n| Form | When |\\n|---|---|\\n| `send name(field: v, …) to target` | the declared type is a record |\\n| `send name(value) to target` | any value assignable to the declared type |\\n| `send name to target` | **only** when the declared type is `Nil` |\\n\\nThe bare form is admitted for `Nil` alone, and it deliberately does not pick up\\na same-named binding.\\n\\nRules:\\n\\n- The name must be declared with `send name: Type` in **this** document's\\n  header. A name declared as an inbound `signal` is refused with a diagnostic\\n  telling you to add the `send` declaration; a document may declare both\\n  directions of the same name.\\n- The target is a **workflow id, which is a `String`** — `workflow.id`, an\\n  input, or any bound `String`. It is not required to be a declared child.\\n- The payload belongs to the signal, not to the target: writing it after `to`\\n  is caught by name.\\n\\nWhat the checker CANNOT prove is the far side's agreement: the target is a\\nruntime id, so an unresolvable target and a target with no matching receiver\\nare runtime facts. A live target that never awaits the signal is the one quiet\\ncase — the signal lands in its history and nothing consumes it.\\n\\n`send` and `to` are positional words, not reserved: `action send(to: String,\\n…)` and `step send` keep working.\\n\\n## 13. The read-only query surface\\n\\n`query` declares what an operator can ask a RUNNING instance, and the type of\\nthe answer. `answer` registers the value, computed from the bindings in scope\\nat that point.\\n\\n```awl\\n//! Review an item, reporting live progress to whoever asks.\\nworkflow gated_review\\n  input item_id: String\\n  input max_passes: Int\\n  query review_status: Status\\n  query passes_left: Int\\n  query heartbeat: Nil\\n  query notes: [String]\\n  query last_note: String?\\n  outcome done: type Status, route success\\n\\ntype Status { phase: String, note: String? }\\n\\nstep announce\\n  answer review_status(phase: \\\"announcing\\\", note: \\\"no reviewer has looked yet\\\")\\n  answer passes_left(max_passes)\\n  answer heartbeat\\n  answer notes([item_id])\\n  answer last_note(item_id)\\n  route done(phase: \\\"announced\\\")\\n```\\n\\n`answer` takes the same payload forms as `send`: named record fields, a bare\\nvalue, or nothing at all when the declared type is `Nil`.\\n\\nAn answer may be re-registered anywhere a statement is legal — inside a `loop`\\nbody, inside `on failure`, in a substep — and re-registering inside a loop is\\nexactly what makes a long-running run report live progress:\\n\\n```awl\\nstep gate after announce\\n  loop verdict = Decision(approved: false)\\n    answer review_status(phase: \\\"reviewing\\\")\\n    evaluate(item: item_id) -> verdict\\n    until verdict.approved\\n    max max_passes\\n  answer review_status(phase: \\\"settled\\\", note: item_id)\\n  outcome settled: when verdict.approved, route done(phase: \\\"settled\\\")\\n  outcome exhausted: otherwise, route gave_up(item: item_id)\\n  on failure\\n    answer review_status(phase: \\\"failed\\\", note: \\\"the gate faulted\\\")\\n    route gave_up(item: item_id)\\n```\\n\\nRules:\\n\\n- Every `answer` must name a query the header declares.\\n- **Every declared query must be answered somewhere in the document**, or the\\n  check fails. The traversal is syntactic, not flow-reachability, and descends\\n  into subflows, fork bodies, loop bodies, substeps, and `on failure` blocks.\\n- The answer's value is checked against the declared type.\\n\\nTwo stated limits: an `answer` in an unreachable step, and an `answer` inside a\\nper-item region that lowers to a synthesized child, both satisfy the\\n\\\"answered\\\" rule while registering nothing an operator querying the parent can\\nsee.\\n\\nOperators read the surface with `aion query <workflow-id> <query-name>`, and\\n`aion awl schema FILE.awl --queries` emits the schema of every answer. A\\ncompleted run no longer answers live queries — inspect a terminal run with\\n`aion describe`.\\n\\n`query` and `answer` are positional words, not reserved: `action\\nparse_output(…, query: String)` and `wait decision -> answer` keep working.\\n\\n## 14. Source proofs\\n\\nEvery shape above is represented in checker-valid repository inputs:\\n\\n- Header/types/workers/pipes: `flagship/valid/awl_hello.awl`.\\n- Worker config and fork modes: `dag-fork/valid/release_pipeline_combined.awl`.\\n- Regions/subflows/visits: `flow-shape/valid/dev_flow.awl`.\\n- Wait/sleep: `step-bodies/valid/wait_and_sleep.awl` and\\n  `step-bodies/valid/wait_timeout_optional.awl`.\\n- Children/spawn: `declarations/valid/child_spawn_combo.awl`.\\n- Loops/outcomes/failure/substeps: `loop-outcomes/valid/*.awl`.\\n- Sending signals: `step-bodies/valid/send_signal.awl`.\\n- Query/answer, every admitted form: `step-bodies/valid/query_answer.awl`.\\n- Declared `run` bodies: `declarations/valid/action_run_bodies.awl`.\\n- The `agent` marker: `declarations/valid/agent_seam.awl`, with\\n  `declarations/valid/agent_seam_unmarked_control.awl` as its control.\\n- The `advisory` marker: `declarations/valid/advisory_side_channel.awl`.\\n- Value route payloads: `flow-shape/valid/value_route_payload.awl`.\\n- Subflow-local names: `flow-shape/valid/subflow_local_names.awl`.\\n- Distribute/collect, strict and tolerant:\\n  `flow-shape/valid/distribute_child_collect.awl` and\\n  `flow-shape/valid/sequence_activity_tolerant.awl`.\\n\\nThe refusals matter as much as the shapes; the sibling `invalid/` directory\\ncarries one fixture per rule (for example\\n`declarations/invalid/run_parameterized_program.awl`,\\n`declarations/invalid/agent_action_declares_a_body.awl`,\\n`step-bodies/invalid/query_declared_never_answered.awl`).\\n\\nThose fixture paths are under `crates/aion-awl/tests/fixtures/rev2/`. Production\\nexamples under `examples/*/awl/` show the same constructs in deployed workflows.\\nRun `aion awl check` on the actual document after every change.\\n\\n## 15. What `aion awl check` does not check\\n\\n`aion awl check` runs the parser and the typechecker, nothing further. The\\ncompile behind `aion awl emit`, `aion deploy`, and `aion run` lowers the\\nchecked document, and lowering refuses a further band of shapes that check\\nclean, each with a diagnostic naming the shape (\\\"does not yet lower …\\\",\\nanchored to a line). A clean check is necessary, not sufficient; the compile\\nhas the final word on these.\\n\\nInside an `on failure` block, lowering refuses a substep, a per-item region, a\\nbounded loop, a fork, a timeout wait (an untimed `wait` lowers), and a child\\nspawn — each named in its diagnostic, for example \\\"a bounded loop inside an\\n`on failure` block\\\". A guarded step whose body leaves \\\"a fallible result the\\n`on failure` edge cannot guard in place (a conditional prelude or an\\noutcome-guard result)\\\" refuses with exactly that phrasing.\\n\\nFork body shapes (§8):\\n\\n- A collection fork body must be exactly one unbound call — \\\"a collection\\n  fork body beyond one unbound call\\\". That one call may name an action or a\\n  child (`dag-fork/valid/child_collection_fork.awl`).\\n- A named-fork branch must be an action call: a child call refuses as \\\"child\\n  calls inside named fork branches\\\", and any other statement as \\\"a named fork\\n  branch beyond an action call\\\".\\n\\nSubsteps (§5):\\n\\n- `after` on a substep parses and checks but refuses at compile — \\\"`after`\\n  dependencies on substeps\\\".\\n- `max … visits` on a substep refuses — \\\"a `max … visits` bound on a substep\\\".\\n- A substep inside a loop body, fork branch, or `on failure` block refuses —\\n  \\\"a substep outside a step body's trailing block\\\". Substeps lower only as the\\n  trailing block of an ordinary step body whose parent carries outcome\\n  clauses.\\n\\nLiteral indexing (`items[0]`) refuses in four positions: a parallel fork\\nbranch (\\\"indexing inside a parallel fork branch\\\" — a `sequential` collection\\nfork accepts it), a parallel per-item track (\\\"indexing inside a parallel\\nper-item track\\\" — `distribute` only; `sequence` tracks accept it), an outcome\\nguard (\\\"indexing inside an outcome guard\\\"), and a visit bound (\\\"indexing\\ninside a `max … visits` bound\\\").\\n\\nThree more:\\n\\n- `sort` requires a comparable key — \\\"`sort` over a non-comparable key (needs\\n  Int, Float, String)\\\". The checker accepts any accessor; comparability is\\n  enforced at compile.\\n- `schema of Type` outside constant position refuses (§2) — bind it to a\\n  `const` and use the constant.\\n- A route targeting a step the plan folded into the middle of a fall-through\\n  chain refuses — \\\"route to a mid-chain step\\\".\\n\\n## 16. Worker documents\\n\\nA `.awl` file whose opening declaration is `worker <queue>` is a **worker\\ndocument**: the implementation of one task queue. It declares that queue's\\nactions, each with the command that runs it, plus the types those actions use,\\nand nothing else.\\n\\n```awl\\n//! Required narration, exactly as a workflow document needs one.\\nworker greeter\\n  action greet(name: String) -> Greeting\\n    run json \\\"greeter-tool --json --name $name\\\"\\n  action shout(text: String) -> String\\n    run text \\\"tr a-z A-Z\\\"\\n  action archive(path: String) -> Archived\\n    run \\\"tar -cf archive.tar $path\\\"\\n\\ntype Greeting { message: String }\\n\\ntype Archived { exit_code: Int, stderr: String }\\n```\\n\\n`aion worker awl <FILE.awl>` serves it. The command runs on the WORKER's host,\\nnot on the server's — that is the difference from a workflow document's\\ndeclared body (§4).\\n\\n### What the family refuses, and why\\n\\n| In a worker document | Refused because |\\n|---|---|\\n| `step`, `child`, `subflow` | A worker document runs no flow. |\\n| `const` | It has no expressions for a compile-time value to feed. |\\n| A second `worker` block | One document serves one queue: the one its opening declaration names. |\\n| An action with no body | A bodyless action is a REQUIREMENT, which a workflow document declares. |\\n| `timeout`, `retry`, `node`, `advisory`, `agent` | Each configures how the CALLER dispatches the action, so each belongs to the action's declaration in the workflow document. |\\n\\nEvery refusal names the remedy. A clean check reports\\n`ok: <file> (worker `<queue>`, N actions)` — there are no steps to count.\\n\\n`aion awl emit`, `aion deploy`, `aion run` and `aion awl schema` (without\\n`--type`) all refuse a worker document by family: it declares no workflow, so\\nthere is no module to emit, nothing to start, and no start contract to derive.\\n\\n### Capture selectors\\n\\nThe word between `run` and the command decides what a SUCCESSFUL command\\nreturns. It decides nothing else — a non-zero exit is retryable with the\\ncommand's stderr, an unspawnable program and a cancellation are terminal, in\\nevery form.\\n\\n| Form | Result | Declared return type |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| `run \\\"…\\\"` | The outcome record `{ exit_code: Int, stdout: String, stderr: String }` | A record over any subset of those members |\\n| `run text \\\"…\\\"` | Trimmed stdout as one string | `String` |\\n| `run json \\\"…\\\"` | Trimmed stdout parsed as JSON | Any declared type except `Nil`; output that does not parse is a TERMINAL failure |\\n\\nInterpolation, the written-out program rule, and the absence of a shell are\\nexactly as §4 states them: one implementation, two families.\\n\\n### The two files, and what holds them honest\\n\\nThe workflow document declares `action greet(name: String) -> Greeting` as a\\nrequirement; the worker document declares the same signature with a body. What\\nkeeps them equal is not diligence but ADMISSION: the worker's advertised\\nsurface is derived through the same seam the deploy path uses, and the server\\nrefuses a worker whose surface differs from the queue's deployed contract,\\nnaming the action and the field. `aion worker awl <FILE.awl> --check` prints\\nthat surface, and the command each action runs, without connecting.\\n\\n**Read `docs/workers/AWL-WORKER-DOCUMENTS.md` before writing one. THERE IS NO\\nSANDBOX** — the same boundaries `docs/workers/DECLARED-COMMANDS.md` states,\\non the worker's host instead of the server's.\\n\\n### The `harness` section\\n\\nA `worker` block may also carry a `harness` section: the launch settings for an\\nAGENT worker serving that queue. It sits before the block's `action`\\ndeclarations, one setting per line. Every word in it — `harness` itself and each\\nfield name — is an ordinary identifier rather than a reserved word, so nothing\\nelse in the language loses those names.\\n\\n```awl\\nworker assistants\\n  harness\\n    kind acp\\n    concurrency 4\\n    reconnect_initial_backoff 5s\\n    reconnect_max_backoff 2m\\n    reconnect_max_attempts 100\\n    env_pass \\\"PATH\\\", \\\"HOME\\\"\\n    command \\\"/usr/local/bin/claude-code-acp\\\"\\n    cwd \\\"/srv/agents/workspace\\\"\\n    permission \\\"deny\\\"\\n    exit_grace 10s\\n  action ask(prompt: String) -> Reply\\n    agent\\n```\\n\\n| Setting | Kind | Value |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| `kind` | both, required | the bare word `acp` or `norn` |\\n| `concurrency` | both, required | a whole number greater than zero |\\n| `reconnect_initial_backoff` | both, required | a positive duration |\\n| `reconnect_max_backoff` | both, required | a positive duration, not shorter than the initial |\\n| `reconnect_max_attempts` | both, required | a whole number greater than zero |\\n| `env_pass` | both, required | quoted variable NAMES, comma-separated |\\n| `command` | `acp` only, required | a quoted path in one of the two forms below |\\n| `args` | both, optional | quoted strings, comma-separated, order preserved |\\n| `cwd` | both, required | a quoted path in one of the two forms below |\\n| `permission` | `acp` only, required | `\\\"deny\\\"` or `\\\"allow-once\\\"` |\\n| `exit_grace` | `acp` only, required | a duration; `0s` is legitimate and means close stdin, then kill at once |\\n| `binary` | `norn` only, optional | a quoted path in one of the two forms below; omitted takes the adapter's own `norn`-on-`PATH` default |\\n\\nThe required set is the DECLARED KIND's. An `acp` section carrying no `binary`\\nis complete, a `norn` section carrying no `command`, `permission` or\\n`exit_grace` is complete, and a setting belonging to the kind the section does\\nnot declare is refused BY NAME rather than ignored — a setting that silently\\ndoes nothing is how an operator comes to believe a policy is in force when it is\\nnot. Nothing is defaulted: `aion awl check` names every required setting the\\nsection omits.\\n\\n`cwd` is required for BOTH kinds, and it has no default. The directory an agent\\nstands in decides which tree it reads and which files it edits — a property of\\nthe work, not of the protocol carrying it — and the only value a default could\\ncarry is whichever directory the worker process happened to be started in,\\nrecorded in no document, argv or log.\\n\\n**The two forms a path setting may take.** Every path-valued setting — `cwd`,\\n`command`, `binary` — is either an ABSOLUTE path, or a value LEADING with the\\nliteral `{workspace_root}`. Anything else is refused, relative paths included: a\\nworker is started from whatever directory a supervisor or a shell happens to be\\nin, so a relative path names a different place on every launch.\\n\\nThe placeholder exists because a document that must be correct on more than one\\nmachine cannot name any absolute literal that is right on the second. The\\nlaunching worker replaces it with THAT box's own workspace root, then holds the\\nresult to the absolute rule before anything is spawned — so a box that cannot\\nresolve a root refuses the launch by name rather than starting an agent\\nsomewhere nobody chose. It must LEAD the value (`{workspace_root}/agents` is a\\npath; `agents/{workspace_root}` could never expand to an absolute one, and is\\nrefused where it is written rather than at the first launch).\\n\\n`env_pass` is the agent subprocess's WHOLE environment. The document names the\\nvariables, the machine running the worker supplies their values, and a variable\\nthe list does not name is absent in the agent whatever the worker's own\\nenvironment carries. Entries are names only; one containing `=` is refused,\\nbecause a value written into a document is a secret in a file that gets\\ncommitted, diffed and deployed.\\n\\nA list does not wrap — a comma at the end of a line is a parse error — and\\n`aion awl fmt` prints the fields in the fixed order above whatever order they\\nwere written in. The section is launch configuration and not flow meaning: a\\ndocument carrying one lowers to the same MIR as the same document without it.\\n\\n**Where it goes.** A `harness` section says how to launch an agent for the\\nactions on a queue that nothing else runs, so a queue whose actions ALL carry\\ndeclared bodies is refused, naming them: there is nothing there for an agent to\\nserve. A worker document requires a body on every action, so in practice the\\nsection lives in the `worker` block of a WORKFLOW document — the one place a\\nbodyless action and a launch can sit together. `aion worker agent <FILE.awl>`\\nreads it, and has no flag for any of the settings in it.\\n\"\n}\n"
 right: "{\n  \"guide\": \"docs/authoring/AWL.md\",\n  \"reference\": \"examples/assistant/resources/AWL-REFERENCE.md\",\n  \"authoring\": \"examples/assistant/resources/AWL-AUTHORING.md\",\n  \"workers\": \"examples/assistant/resources/WORKERS.md\",\n  \"commands\": \"examples/assistant/resources/COMMANDS.md\",\n  \"declared_commands\": \"docs/workers/DECLARED-COMMANDS.md\",\n  \"forms\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"declaration\",\n      \"label\": \"Declaration\",\n      \"description\": \"Words that may open a declaration at the start of a top-level line.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"label\": \"Header clause\",\n      \"description\": \"Words that stand in a workflow header or a worker block, qualifying the declaration above them.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"statement\",\n      \"label\": \"Step statement\",\n      \"description\": \"Words that open or continue a statement inside a step or subflow body.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"pipeline-stage\",\n      \"label\": \"Pipeline stage\",\n      \"description\": \"Words that stand after |> as a stage of a value pipeline.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"condition-word\",\n      \"label\": \"Condition word\",\n      \"description\": \"Words that stand inside a when condition.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"value-word\",\n      \"label\": \"Value word\",\n      \"description\": \"Words that stand inside a value expression: opening a declaration's value after =, following schema, or naming run's capture form.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"target-word\",\n      \"label\": \"Target word\",\n      \"description\": \"Words that stand only where a statement names its target: a route's destination, an ordering dependency, or a signal's addressee.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"positions\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"top-level\",\n      \"label\": \"at the start of a top-level line\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"workflow-header\",\n      \"label\": \"in a workflow header\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"worker-block\",\n      \"label\": \"in a worker block\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"step-body\",\n      \"label\": \"in a step or subflow body\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"type-body\",\n      \"label\": \"in a type body\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"route-target\",\n      \"label\": \"after route, as its target\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"step-reference\",\n      \"label\": \"after after, naming a step\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"signal-reference\",\n      \"label\": \"after wait or send, naming a signal\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"pipe-stage\",\n      \"label\": \"after |>, as a pipe stage\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"type-reference\",\n      \"label\": \"after a colon, naming a type\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"condition\",\n      \"label\": \"in a when condition\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"const-value\",\n      \"label\": \"after = in a const declaration\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"type-door\",\n      \"label\": \"after = in a type declaration\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"schema-source\",\n      \"label\": \"after schema in a value expression\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"run-capture\",\n      \"label\": \"after run in a declared action body\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"entries\": [\n    {\n      \"word\": \"workflow\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Opens the document: one `.awl` file declares exactly one workflow, named here.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Greet whoever starts the run.\\nworkflow greet_visitor\\n  input name: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep greet\\n  \\\"hello \\\" + name -> greeting\\n  route done(greeting)\",\n        \"//! `workflow.id` names this very run — here handed to a coordinator.\\nworkflow register_run\\n  input coordinator_id: String\\n  send registered: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep register\\n  send registered(workflow.id) to coordinator_id\\n  route done(workflow.id)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 1,\n        \"title\": \"Document structure\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §1 — Document structure\",\n      \"form\": \"declaration\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"top-level\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"input\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares one field of the workflow's start contract; it is repeatable.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Each `input` line declares one field of the start contract.\\nworkflow open_ticket\\n  input title: String\\n  input urgent: Bool\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep file\\n  outcome fast: when urgent, route done(\\\"expedited: \\\" + title)\\n  outcome slow: otherwise, route done(\\\"queued: \\\" + title)\",\n        \"//! Inputs can be records and lists, typed like any other value.\\nworkflow plan_rollout\\n  input change: Change\\n  input hosts: [String]\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Change { title: String }\\n\\nstep plan\\n  hosts |> count -> planned\\n  outcome big: when planned > 3, route done(\\\"staged rollout of \\\" + change.title)\\n  outcome small: otherwise, route done(\\\"direct rollout of \\\" + change.title)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 3,\n        \"title\": \"Workflow header\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §3 — Workflow header\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"workflow-header\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"signal\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares a durable signal this workflow receives; a step body takes delivery with `wait`.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Hold until the declared approval signal arrives.\\nworkflow release_gate\\n  input build_id: String\\n  signal approval: Approval\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Approval { reviewer: String }\\n\\nstep hold\\n  wait approval -> decision\\n  route done(decision.reviewer + \\\" approved \\\" + build_id)\",\n        \"//! Two declared signals: a review verdict, then a go-ahead to ship.\\nworkflow two_gate_release\\n  input build_id: String\\n  signal verdict: Verdict\\n  signal go: Go\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Verdict { approved: Bool }\\ntype Go      { operator: String }\\n\\nstep review\\n  wait verdict -> review_verdict\\n\\nstep ship\\n  wait go -> clearance\\n  outcome shipped: when review_verdict.approved, route done(clearance.operator)\\n  outcome held: otherwise, route done(\\\"held: \\\" + build_id)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 3,\n        \"title\": \"Workflow header\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §3 — Workflow header\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"workflow-header\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"outcome\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares a terminal payload in the workflow header, and a guarded arm inside a step body.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `outcome` declares terminals in the header and guarded arms in a step.\\nworkflow triage_alert\\n  input urgent: Bool\\n  outcome paged:  type String, route success\\n  outcome logged: type String, route success\\n\\nstep decide\\n  outcome page: when urgent, route paged(\\\"paged the operator\\\")\\n  outcome log: otherwise, route logged(\\\"logged for morning\\\")\",\n        \"//! A subflow declares exactly one typed outcome of its own.\\nworkflow audit_repo\\n  input repo: String\\n  outcome done: type Audit, route success\\n\\ntype Audit { clean: Bool }\\n\\nworker auditors\\n  action scan(target: String) -> Audit\\n\\nsubflow audit_once(target: String)\\n  outcome out: type Audit\\n\\n  step look\\n    scan(target: target) -> audit\\n    route out(audit)\\n\\nstep audit\\n  audit_once(target: repo) -> audit\\n\\nstep finish\\n  audit |> route done\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 7,\n        \"title\": \"Selection, outcomes, and routes\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §7 — Selection, outcomes, and routes\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"workflow-header\",\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"type\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares a record, an enum, or a schema door, and names the type an `outcome` carries.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Declare an enum and a record, then build one for the outcome.\\nworkflow triage_ticket\\n  input note: String\\n  outcome done: type Triage, route success\\n\\ntype Category = Urgent | Routine\\ntype Triage { category: Category, note: String }\\n\\nstep judge\\n  route done(category: Routine, note: note)\",\n        \"//! `///` documents a declaration; optional fields are `T?`.\\nworkflow file_finding\\n  input title: String\\n  outcome done: type Finding, route success\\n\\n/// One reviewer finding.\\ntype Finding {\\n  title: String,\\n  /// Absent until a reviewer writes one.\\n  note: String?,\\n}\\n\\nstep file\\n  route done(title: title)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 2,\n        \"title\": \"Values and types\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §2 — Values and types\",\n      \"form\": \"declaration\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"top-level\",\n        \"workflow-header\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"schema\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Opens a schema door: `schema(\\\"path\\\")` imports one, `schema { … }` inlines one, and `schema of Type` derives one in constant position.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! An inline schema door names a JSON shape as an AWL type.\\nworkflow ingest_payload\\n  input raw: Payload\\n  outcome done: type Payload, route success\\n\\ntype Payload = schema {\\n  \\\"type\\\": \\\"object\\\",\\n  \\\"required\\\": [\\\"value\\\"],\\n  \\\"properties\\\": { \\\"value\\\": { \\\"type\\\": \\\"string\\\" } }\\n}\\n\\nstep accept\\n  route done(raw)\",\n        \"//! `schema of Type` in constant position, handed to an agent action.\\nworkflow structured_reply\\n  input question: String\\n  outcome done: type Answer, route success\\n\\nconst answer_schema = schema of Answer\\n\\ntype Answer { reply: String }\\n\\nworker assistants\\n  action ask(prompt: String, output_schema: String) -> Answer\\n\\nstep ask\\n  ask(prompt: question, output_schema: answer_schema) -> result\\n  route done(result)\",\n        \"//! Import a schema file as a type; the path resolves beside the document.\\nworkflow ingest_existing\\n  input raw: Existing\\n  outcome done: type Existing, route success\\n\\ntype Existing = schema(\\\"schemas/existing.schema.json\\\")\\n\\nstep accept\\n  route done(raw)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 2,\n        \"title\": \"Values and types\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §2 — Values and types\",\n      \"form\": \"value-word\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"const-value\",\n        \"type-door\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"worker\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares a typed worker contract whose name is the task queue; it is a requirement, never an implementation.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! The worker block is a typed requirement on the `greeter` task queue.\\nworkflow greet_politely\\n  input name: String\\n  outcome done: type Greeting, route success\\n\\ntype Greeting { text: String }\\n\\nworker greeter\\n  action greet(name: String) -> Greeting\\n\\nstep greet\\n  greet(name: name) -> reply\\n  route done(reply)\",\n        \"//! A worker DOCUMENT: the implementation of one task queue.\\nworker greeter\\n  action greet(name: String) -> Greeting\\n    run json \\\"greeter-tool --json --name $name\\\"\\n\\ntype Greeting { message: String }\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"declaration\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"top-level\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"action\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares one wire contract on a worker: named parameters, one return type, and at most one config line.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Each action line is one wire contract: named parameters, one return.\\nworkflow inspect_repo\\n  input repo: String\\n  outcome done: type Report, route success\\n\\ntype Report { summary: String }\\n\\nworker inspectors\\n  action inspect(repo: String) -> Report\\n\\nstep inspect\\n  inspect(repo: repo) -> report\\n  route done(report)\",\n        \"//! In a worker document every action carries the command that runs it.\\nworker archiver\\n  action archive(path: String) -> Archived\\n    run \\\"tar -cf archive.tar $path\\\"\\n\\ntype Archived { exit_code: Int, stderr: String }\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"child\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares a separate durable workflow this one may call and await.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Call a separate durable workflow and await its result.\\nworkflow grade_essay\\n  input essay: String\\n  outcome done: type Grade, route success\\n\\ntype Grade { score: Int }\\n\\nchild score_essay(essay: String) -> Grade\\n\\nstep grade\\n  score_essay(essay: essay) -> grade\\n  route done(grade)\",\n        \"//! Await one child; detach another this run never waits for.\\nworkflow grade_and_notify\\n  input essay: String\\n  outcome done: type Grade, route success\\n\\ntype Grade { score: Int }\\n\\nchild score_essay(essay: String) -> Grade\\nchild notify_graded(score: Int) -> Nil\\n\\nstep grade\\n  score_essay(essay: essay) -> grade\\n  spawn notify_graded(score: grade.score)\\n  route done(grade)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 5,\n        \"title\": \"Children and subflows\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §5 — Children and subflows\",\n      \"form\": \"declaration\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"top-level\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"step\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares one node of the step graph; the statements inside it run in written order.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Statements inside a step run in written order; steps chain the graph.\\nworkflow compose_greeting\\n  input name: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep compose\\n  \\\"hello \\\" + name -> greeting\\n\\nstep deliver\\n  route done(greeting)\",\n        \"//! A body-less step of only outcomes is a pure decision.\\nworkflow sort_ticket\\n  input urgent: Bool\\n  outcome fast: type String, route success\\n  outcome slow: type String, route success\\n\\nstep triage\\n  outcome now: when urgent, route fast(\\\"page\\\")\\n  outcome later: otherwise, route slow(\\\"queue\\\")\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 6,\n        \"title\": \"Steps, dependencies, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §6 — Steps, dependencies, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"declaration\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"top-level\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"after\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Names the steps a step waits for, so the dependency is written rather than inferred from position.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `verify` waits for `build`: the dependency is written, not inferred.\\nworkflow gated_build\\n  input tag: String\\n  outcome done: type Checked, route success\\n\\ntype Built   { artifact: String }\\ntype Checked { ok: Bool }\\n\\nworker ci\\n  action build(tag: String) -> Built\\n  action verify(artifact: String) -> Checked\\n\\nstep build\\n  build(tag: tag) -> built\\n\\nstep verify after build\\n  verify(artifact: built.artifact) -> checked\\n  route done(checked)\",\n        \"//! A diamond: build and notes run independently, publish waits for both.\\nworkflow release_notes\\n  input tag: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Build { artifact: String }\\ntype Notes { text: String }\\n\\nworker release\\n  action build(tag: String) -> Build\\n  action render_notes(tag: String) -> Notes\\n\\nstep start\\n  \\\"release \\\" + tag -> label\\n\\nstep build after start\\n  build(tag: tag) -> built\\n\\nstep notes after start\\n  render_notes(tag: tag) -> rendered\\n\\nstep publish after build, notes\\n  route done(label + \\\" \\\" + built.artifact + rendered.text)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 6,\n        \"title\": \"Steps, dependencies, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §6 — Steps, dependencies, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"fork\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Runs work concurrently inside one step: `fork item in items` one branch per item, or a bare `fork` for named branches.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! One branch per repo, checked in parallel, joined in input order.\\nworkflow sweep_repos\\n  input repos: [String]\\n  outcome done: type [Verdict], route success\\n\\ntype Verdict { ok: Bool }\\n\\nworker ci\\n  action check(repo: String) -> Verdict\\n\\nstep sweep\\n  fork repo in repos\\n    check(repo: repo)\\n  join -> verdicts\\n  route done(verdicts)\",\n        \"//! A bare fork runs named heterogeneous calls concurrently.\\nworkflow load_dashboard\\n  input user_id: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Profile { name: String }\\ntype History { entries: Int }\\n\\nworker accounts\\n  action fetch_profile(id: String) -> Profile\\n  action fetch_history(id: String) -> History\\n\\nstep load\\n  fork\\n    fetch_profile(id: user_id) -> profile\\n    fetch_history(id: user_id) -> history\\n  join\\n  outcome busy: when history.entries > 3, route done(profile.name + \\\" is busy\\\")\\n  outcome quiet: otherwise, route done(profile.name)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 8,\n        \"title\": \"Parallel and sequential work\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §8 — Parallel and sequential work\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"join\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Closes a `fork`, binding a collection fork's results in input order.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `join` closes the fork, binding the branches' results in input order.\\nworkflow gather_reports\\n  input repos: [String]\\n  outcome done: type [Report], route success\\n\\ntype Report { summary: String }\\n\\nworker inspectors\\n  action inspect(repo: String) -> Report\\n\\nstep gather\\n  fork repo in repos\\n    inspect(repo: repo)\\n  join -> reports\\n  route done(reports)\",\n        \"//! Bare `join` closes named branches; there is no collection to bind.\\nworkflow warm_caches\\n  input region: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nworker infra\\n  action warm_dns(region: String) -> Nil\\n  action warm_cdn(region: String) -> Nil\\n\\nstep warm\\n  fork\\n    warm_dns(region: region)\\n    warm_cdn(region: region)\\n  join\\n  route done(region)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 8,\n        \"title\": \"Parallel and sequential work\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §8 — Parallel and sequential work\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"loop\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Runs a bounded value-threading loop whose body runs at least once and must rebind the threaded value.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Improve the draft until it is ready, at most three passes.\\nworkflow refine_draft\\n  input topic: String\\n  outcome done:  type Draft,  route success\\n  outcome stuck: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Draft { body: String, ready: Bool }\\n\\nworker writers\\n  action improve(topic: String, prior: Draft) -> Draft\\n\\nstep refine\\n  loop draft = Draft(body: \\\"\\\", ready: false)\\n    improve(topic: topic, prior: draft) -> draft\\n    until draft.ready\\n    max 3\\n  outcome ready: when draft.ready, route done(draft)\\n  outcome exhausted: otherwise, route stuck(\\\"the draft never settled\\\")\",\n        \"//! The threaded value carries state between passes; rounds are counted.\\nworkflow tune_model\\n  input target: String\\n  outcome done:  type Int,    route success\\n  outcome stuck: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Tuning { score: Int, good_enough: Bool }\\n\\nworker lab\\n  action tune(target: String, prior: Tuning) -> Tuning\\n\\nstep tune\\n  loop tuning = Tuning(score: 0, good_enough: false) counting rounds\\n    tune(target: target, prior: tuning) -> tuning\\n    until tuning.good_enough\\n    max 8\\n  outcome tuned: when tuning.good_enough, route done(rounds)\\n  outcome gave_up: otherwise, route stuck(target)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 9,\n        \"title\": \"Bounded iteration\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §9 — Bounded iteration\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"counting\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Binds the number of completed iterations of a `loop`.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `counting` binds how many iterations the loop completed.\\nworkflow poll_job\\n  input job: String\\n  outcome done:  type Int,    route success\\n  outcome stuck: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Status { ready: Bool }\\n\\nworker jobs\\n  action poll(job: String, prior: Status) -> Status\\n\\nstep watch\\n  loop status = Status(ready: false) counting polls\\n    poll(job: job, prior: status) -> status\\n    until status.ready\\n    max 10\\n  outcome ready: when status.ready, route done(polls)\\n  outcome gave_up: otherwise, route stuck(job)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 9,\n        \"title\": \"Bounded iteration\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §9 — Bounded iteration\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"until\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"States the condition that ends a `loop`, tested after the body has run.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `until` ends the loop, tested after each pass of the body.\\nworkflow stabilize_build\\n  input tag: String\\n  outcome done:  type Build,  route success\\n  outcome stuck: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Build { green: Bool }\\n\\nworker ci\\n  action rebuild(tag: String, prior: Build) -> Build\\n\\nstep converge\\n  loop build = Build(green: false)\\n    rebuild(tag: tag, prior: build) -> build\\n    until build.green\\n    max 5\\n  outcome green: when build.green, route done(build)\\n  outcome exhausted: otherwise, route stuck(tag)\",\n        \"//! A compound condition: stop only when ready AND approved.\\nworkflow draft_and_signoff\\n  input topic: String\\n  outcome done:  type Draft,  route success\\n  outcome stuck: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Draft { ready: Bool, approved: Bool }\\n\\nworker desks\\n  action revise(topic: String, prior: Draft) -> Draft\\n\\nstep converge\\n  loop draft = Draft(ready: false, approved: false)\\n    revise(topic: topic, prior: draft) -> draft\\n    until draft.ready and draft.approved\\n    max 6\\n  outcome settled: when draft.ready and draft.approved, route done(draft)\\n  outcome exhausted: otherwise, route stuck(topic)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 9,\n        \"title\": \"Bounded iteration\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §9 — Bounded iteration\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"max\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Bounds iteration: the mandatory ceiling on a `loop`, or `max N visits` on a step inside a flow loop.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `max` is the mandatory ceiling; exhaustion is a distinct outcome.\\nworkflow negotiate_quote\\n  input request: String\\n  outcome done:  type Offer,  route success\\n  outcome stuck: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Offer { accepted: Bool }\\n\\nworker brokers\\n  action haggle(request: String, prior: Offer) -> Offer\\n\\nstep negotiate\\n  loop offer = Offer(accepted: false)\\n    haggle(request: request, prior: offer) -> offer\\n    until offer.accepted\\n    max 4\\n  outcome agreed: when offer.accepted, route done(offer)\\n  outcome exhausted: otherwise, route stuck(request)\",\n        \"//! The other place `max` stands: a visit bound on a step in a flow loop.\\nworkflow polish_copy\\n  input text: String\\n  outcome done: type Verdict, route success\\n\\ntype Verdict { ok: Bool }\\n\\nworker desks\\n  action review(text: String) -> Verdict\\n\\nstep review\\n  review(text: text) -> verdict\\n\\nstep judge\\n  outcome again: when not verdict.ok and visits < 2, route review\\n  outcome settled: otherwise, route done(verdict)\\n  max 2 visits\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 9,\n        \"title\": \"Bounded iteration\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §9 — Bounded iteration\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"sequential\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Runs a collection `fork` one item at a time in input order instead of in parallel.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `sequential` publishes one target at a time, in input order.\\nworkflow publish_targets\\n  input targets: [String]\\n  outcome done: type [Receipt], route success\\n\\ntype Receipt { target: String }\\n\\nworker publisher\\n  action publish(target: String) -> Receipt\\n\\nstep publish_all\\n  fork target in targets sequential\\n    publish(target: target)\\n  join -> receipts\\n  route done(receipts)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 8,\n        \"title\": \"Parallel and sequential work\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §8 — Parallel and sequential work\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"spawn\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Starts a child workflow without waiting for it, so it can bind no result.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Start a child without waiting; it can bind no result.\\nworkflow archive_report\\n  input report_id: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nchild notify_archived(report_id: String) -> Nil\\n\\nstep archive\\n  spawn notify_archived(report_id: report_id)\\n  route done(report_id)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 5,\n        \"title\": \"Children and subflows\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §5 — Children and subflows\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"wait\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Waits for a declared signal and binds it; a `timeout` makes that binding optional, because expiry is absence.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Take delivery of the declared signal, binding its payload.\\nworkflow hold_for_go\\n  input build_id: String\\n  signal go: Approval\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Approval { approver: String }\\n\\nstep hold\\n  wait go -> approval\\n  route done(approval.approver + \\\" released \\\" + build_id)\",\n        \"//! A timed wait binds an optional, because expiry is absence.\\nworkflow ask_signoff\\n  input change_id: String\\n  signal signoff: Signoff\\n  outcome shipped:   type String, route success\\n  outcome escalated: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Signoff { reviewer: String }\\n\\nstep hold\\n  wait signoff timeout 2d -> decision\\n  outcome approved: when decision is present, route shipped(decision.reviewer)\\n  outcome lapsed: otherwise, route escalated(\\\"lapsed: \\\" + change_id)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 10,\n        \"title\": \"Waits, timeouts, signals, and sleep\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §10 — Waits, timeouts, signals, and sleep\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"sleep\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Sleeps for a duration on a durable timer.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Sleep on a durable timer; the run survives a restart mid-sleep.\\nworkflow cool_down\\n  input build_id: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep pause\\n  sleep 30s\\n  route done(build_id)\",\n        \"//! Sleep between polls: a durable timer inside the loop body.\\nworkflow await_export\\n  input job: String\\n  outcome done:  type Status, route success\\n  outcome stuck: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Status { finished: Bool }\\n\\nworker exports\\n  action poll(job: String, prior: Status) -> Status\\n\\nstep watch\\n  loop status = Status(finished: false)\\n    sleep 30s\\n    poll(job: job, prior: status) -> status\\n    until status.finished\\n    max 20\\n  outcome finished: when status.finished, route done(status)\\n  outcome timed_out: otherwise, route stuck(job)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 10,\n        \"title\": \"Waits, timeouts, signals, and sleep\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §10 — Waits, timeouts, signals, and sleep\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"timeout\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Bounds the workflow run in the header, one activity on an action or call, or one `wait` — three distinct places.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! The header timeout bounds the whole run.\\nworkflow nightly_sweep\\n  timeout 2h\\n  input target: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep sweep\\n  route done(\\\"swept \\\" + target)\",\n        \"//! An activity timeout on the declaration, tightened at one call site.\\nworkflow build_twice\\n  input tag: String\\n  outcome done: type Build, route success\\n\\ntype Build { artifact: String }\\n\\nworker builders\\n  action build(tag: String) -> Build\\n    timeout 30m\\n\\nstep quick_probe\\n  build(tag: tag) -> probe\\n    timeout 5m\\n\\nstep full_build\\n  build(tag: probe.artifact) -> built\\n  route done(built)\",\n        \"//! A wait with a timeout binds an optional: expiry is absence.\\nworkflow hold_for_signoff\\n  input change_id: String\\n  signal signoff: Signoff\\n  outcome shipped:   type String, route success\\n  outcome escalated: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Signoff { reviewer: String }\\n\\nstep hold\\n  wait signoff timeout 2d -> decision\\n  outcome approved: when decision is present, route shipped(decision.reviewer)\\n  outcome lapsed: otherwise, route escalated(\\\"window lapsed: \\\" + change_id)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"workflow-header\",\n        \"worker-block\",\n        \"step-body\",\n        \"signal-reference\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"retry\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Sets an action's retry policy, as `retry N every DURATION` or `retry N backoff MIN..MAX`.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Retry the flaky pull three times before the step faults.\\nworkflow sync_mirror\\n  input remote: String\\n  outcome done: type Synced, route success\\n\\ntype Synced { revision: String }\\n\\nworker mirrors\\n  action pull(remote: String) -> Synced\\n    retry 3 every 30s\\n\\nstep pull\\n  pull(remote: remote) -> synced\\n  route done(synced)\",\n        \"//! The growing form: intervals stretch from five seconds toward four minutes.\\nworkflow crawl_source\\n  input source: String\\n  outcome done: type Page, route success\\n\\ntype Page { body: String }\\n\\nworker crawlers\\n  action crawl(source: String) -> Page\\n    retry 6 backoff 5s..4m\\n\\nstep crawl\\n  crawl(source: source) -> page\\n  route done(page)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"fallback\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Declares the ordered task-queue chain consulted after a provider policy refusal.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Route policy refusals through the declared queue chain.\\nworkflow route_provider\\n  input prompt: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nworker agents\\n  action run(prompt: String) -> String\\n    fallback norn, claude\\n\\nstep run\\n  run(prompt: prompt) -> answer\\n  route done(answer)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"advisory\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Marks an action whose failure never faults its calling step; it can bind no result and be no pipe stage.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! An advisory announcement can fail without faulting the run.\\nworkflow ship_build\\n  input version: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nworker notifier\\n  action announce(version: String) -> Nil\\n    advisory, timeout 30s\\n\\nstep ship\\n  announce(version: version)\\n  route done(version)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"agent\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Marks an action as an agent seam: its one `String` parameter carries a prompt and its declared result carries accumulated `text`, last-answer `final_message`, `stop_reason`, and `session_id`.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! An agent action hands its prompt to a harness and binds the outcome\\n//! record: accumulated `text`, the last answer as `final_message`, the canonical\\n//! `stop_reason`, and the `session_id` handle of the conversation that produced it.\\nworkflow ask_reviewer\\n  input question: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Reply { text: String, final_message: String, stop_reason: String, session_id: String }\\n\\nworker assistants\\n  action ask(prompt: String) -> Reply\\n    agent, timeout 10m\\n\\nstep ask\\n  ask(prompt: question) -> reply\\n  route done(reply.text)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"every\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Sets the fixed interval between retries in `retry N every DURATION`.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `every` fixes the interval between retries: ten seconds, each time.\\nworkflow ping_service\\n  input url: String\\n  outcome done: type Pong, route success\\n\\ntype Pong { latency_ms: Int }\\n\\nworker probes\\n  action ping(url: String) -> Pong\\n    retry 5 every 10s\\n\\nstep ping\\n  ping(url: url) -> pong\\n  route done(pong)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"backoff\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Sets the growing retry interval in `retry N backoff MIN..MAX`.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `backoff` grows the retry interval from ten seconds toward two minutes.\\nworkflow fetch_upstream\\n  input source: String\\n  outcome done: type Fetched, route success\\n\\ntype Fetched { bytes: Int }\\n\\nworker fetchers\\n  action fetch(source: String) -> Fetched\\n    retry 4 backoff 10s..2m\\n\\nstep fetch\\n  fetch(source: source) -> fetched\\n  route done(fetched)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"node\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Pins dispatch to a named worker node, on the action declaration or overridden at a call site.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Pin an action's dispatch to the named worker node.\\nworkflow build_release\\n  input tag: String\\n  outcome done: type Build, route success\\n\\ntype Build { artifact: String }\\n\\nworker builders\\n  action build(tag: String) -> Build\\n    node linux_large\\n\\nstep build\\n  build(tag: tag) -> built\\n  route done(built)\",\n        \"//! Override the declared node at one urgent call site.\\nworkflow hotfix_build\\n  input tag: String\\n  outcome done: type Build, route success\\n\\ntype Build { artifact: String }\\n\\nworker builders\\n  action build(tag: String) -> Build\\n    node standard, timeout 30m\\n\\nstep build\\n  build(tag: tag) -> built\\n    node urgent, timeout 5m\\n  route done(built)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 4,\n        \"title\": \"Workers, actions, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §4 — Workers, actions, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"worker-block\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"on\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Opens `on failure`, the block a step runs when it faults, which must itself end in a route.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `on failure` runs when the step faults, and must end in a route.\\nworkflow publish_package\\n  input name: String\\n  outcome done:   type String, route success\\n  outcome failed: type String, route failure\\n\\nworker registry\\n  action publish(name: String) -> Nil\\n  action yank(name: String) -> Nil\\n\\nstep publish\\n  publish(name: name)\\n  route done(name)\\n\\n  on failure\\n    yank(name: name)\\n    route failed(\\\"publish failed; yanked\\\")\",\n        \"//! Compensation may route back into the graph, not only to an outcome.\\nworkflow deploy_with_rollback\\n  input build: String\\n  outcome done:   type String, route success\\n  outcome failed: type String, route failure\\n\\nworker ops\\n  action deploy(build: String) -> Nil\\n  action rollback(build: String) -> Nil\\n  action report(build: String) -> Nil\\n\\nstep deploy\\n  deploy(build: build)\\n  route done(build)\\n\\n  on failure\\n    rollback(build: build)\\n    route notify\\n\\nstep notify\\n  report(build: build)\\n  route failed(\\\"deploy failed; rolled back: \\\" + build)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 11,\n        \"title\": \"Failure handling\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §11 — Failure handling\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"failure\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Maps a declared outcome to the engine's failed status, and after `on` names a step's compensation block.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `route failure` marks the outcome that fails the run.\\nworkflow validate_order\\n  input order_id: String\\n  input valid: Bool\\n  outcome accepted: type String, route success\\n  outcome rejected: type String, route failure\\n\\nstep validate\\n  outcome ok: when valid, route accepted(order_id)\\n  outcome bad: otherwise, route rejected(\\\"invalid order: \\\" + order_id)\",\n        \"//! After `on`, the same word names a step's compensation block.\\nworkflow guard_import\\n  input dataset: String\\n  outcome done:   type String, route success\\n  outcome failed: type String, route failure\\n\\nworker importers\\n  action import_rows(dataset: String) -> Nil\\n\\nstep import\\n  import_rows(dataset: dataset)\\n  route done(dataset)\\n\\n  on failure\\n    route failed(\\\"import faulted: \\\" + dataset)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 3,\n        \"title\": \"Workflow header\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §3 — Workflow header\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"workflow-header\",\n        \"step-body\",\n        \"route-target\",\n        \"step-reference\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"when\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Guards an outcome arm; the first true guard wins, and its proofs narrow optionals through that arm.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! The first true `when` wins; its proofs hold through the arm.\\nworkflow admit_finding\\n  input finding: Finding\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Finding { blocking: Bool, detail: String? }\\n\\nstep judge\\n  outcome real: when finding.detail is present, route done(finding.detail)\\n  outcome noise: otherwise, route done(\\\"no detail recorded\\\")\",\n        \"//! Guards over every enum variant are total; no `otherwise` needed.\\nworkflow route_ticket\\n  input ticket: Ticket\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\ntype Category = Urgent | Routine | Spam\\ntype Ticket { category: Category }\\n\\nstep route_it\\n  outcome page: when ticket.category == Urgent, route done(\\\"page\\\")\\n  outcome queue: when ticket.category == Routine, route done(\\\"queue\\\")\\n  outcome bin: when ticket.category == Spam, route done(\\\"discard\\\")\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 7,\n        \"title\": \"Selection, outcomes, and routes\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §7 — Selection, outcomes, and routes\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"step-body\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"otherwise\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"The complement arm of a guarded outcome set, which must come last.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `otherwise` is the complement arm, and must come last.\\nworkflow rate_change\\n  input risky: Bool\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep rate\\n  outcome careful: when risky, route done(\\\"hold for review\\\")\\n  outcome fine: otherwise, route done(\\\"ship it\\\")\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 7,\n        \"title\": \"Selection, outcomes, and routes\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §7 — Selection, outcomes, and routes\",\n      \"form\": \"condition-word\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"condition\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"route\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Sends flow to another step or to a declared outcome, supplying that outcome's payload.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `route` sends flow to another step or to a declared outcome.\\nworkflow escalate_alert\\n  input urgent: Bool\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep assess\\n  outcome page: when urgent, route page_operator\\n  outcome log: otherwise, route done(\\\"logged\\\")\\n\\nstep page_operator\\n  route done(\\\"paged\\\")\",\n        \"//! A backward route makes a flow loop; the cycle carries a visit bound.\\nworkflow retry_gate\\n  input repo: String\\n  outcome done:   type Verdict, route success\\n  outcome failed: type String,  route failure\\n\\ntype Verdict { ok: Bool }\\n\\nworker ci\\n  action check(repo: String) -> Verdict\\n\\nstep attempt\\n  check(repo: repo) -> verdict\\n\\nstep judge\\n  outcome again: when not verdict.ok and visits < 3, route attempt\\n  outcome good: when verdict.ok, route done(verdict)\\n  outcome exhausted: otherwise, route failed(repo)\\n  max 3 visits\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 7,\n        \"title\": \"Selection, outcomes, and routes\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §7 — Selection, outcomes, and routes\",\n      \"form\": \"statement\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"workflow-header\",\n        \"step-body\",\n        \"pipe-stage\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"success\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Maps a declared outcome to the engine's completed status.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! `route success` maps the outcome to the engine's completed status.\\nworkflow finish_job\\n  input job: String\\n  outcome done: type String, route success\\n\\nstep wrap\\n  route done(\\\"finished \\\" + job)\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 3,\n        \"title\": \"Workflow header\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §3 — Workflow header\",\n      \"form\": \"header-clause\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"workflow-header\",\n        \"route-target\",\n        \"step-reference\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"filter\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Keeps the elements of a collection its predicate accepts.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Keep only the findings the predicate accepts.\\nworkflow keep_blockers\\n  input findings: [Finding]\\n  outcome done: type [Finding], route success\\n\\ntype Finding { title: String, blocking: Bool }\\n\\nstep sift\\n  findings |> filter(.blocking) -> blockers\\n  route done(blockers)\",\n        \"//! A filter stage inside a longer pipe, feeding a tally.\\nworkflow measure_noise\\n  input findings: [Finding]\\n  outcome done:   type String, route success\\n  outcome failed: type String, route failure\\n\\ntype Finding { resolved: Bool }\\n\\nstep measure\\n  findings |> filter(.resolved) |> count -> settled\\n  findings |> count -> total\\n  outcome all_settled: when settled == total, route done(\\\"all settled\\\")\\n  outcome open_items: otherwise, route failed(\\\"unresolved findings remain\\\")\"\n      ],\n      \"section\": {\n        \"number\": 6,\n        \"title\": \"Steps, dependencies, and calls\"\n      },\n      \"citation\": \"AWL reference §6 — Steps, dependencies, and calls\",\n      \"form\": \"pipeline-stage\",\n      \"positions\": [\n        \"pipe-stage\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"word\": \"map\",\n      \"kind\": \"keyword\",\n      \"summary\": \"Rewrites each element of a collection through a deterministic value expression.\",\n      \"examples\": [\n        \"//! Rewrite each element through a deterministic expression.\\nworkflow list_titles\\n  input findings: [Finding]\\n  outcome done: type [String], route success\\n\\ntype Finding { title: String, blocking: Bool }\\n\\nstep rename\\n  findings |> map(.title) -> titles\\n  route done(titles)\",\n        \"//!
